The NFC South is football’s annual participation trophy

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The belief that the NFC South would be better in 2026 was completely delusional. If we look back to the close of the 2025 season, there was a lot to look forward to when it came to the division. The Falcons looked like they were going to take a step forward with Kevin Stefanski and Bijan Robinson. Carolina looked like a team that had begun to put it together on the back of the Bryce Young/Tetairoa McMillan handcuff. The Saints had a big free agency that appeared to set the table for a Tyler Shough breakout. While the Buccaneers, steady as ever, had excellent defensive talent and a good enough offense.

Fast forward to today, and once more the NFC South is hilarious. The NFL's only remaining division that doesn't have a single team above .500, it will again host a home playoff game where a much better team will have to play visitor, and will continue to both be laughed at, as well as stoke the flames of frustration in the playoff process.

Someone has to win this division, so who's it going to be?

Current record: 3-7Remaining SoS (lower is better): -0.2Future opponents: Vikings, Lions, Browns, Commanders, Buccaneers, Saints, PanthersProjected finish: 5-12

The Falcons decided to punt on the season when they traded away Jessie Bates, and it's probably smart that they did. This team is just not in a position to compete, and there's more soul searching to be done at the quarterback position moving forward.

There's only one real bright spot for Atlanta on offense, which is Bijan Robinson. We know Drake London is a hell of a receiver, but he just can't show it with the horrible quarterbacking he has to work with. Sure, he'll finish with more than 1,000 yards receiving because he's a beast, but right now he only has one receiving touchdown because this team can't pass the ball in the red zone. Some of that is a focus on running the ball, but a receiver with London's frame should be able to do more.

We just didn't see the defense take the step forward this season we assumed they could. Atlanta ranks 23rd in the NFL in points allowed, they're 25th against the pass, and 19th against the run. There's nothing this team does well, and without a consistent passing game there's no mechanism to generate enough explosive plays to get back in games.

Current record: 4-5Remaining SoS (lower is better): -2.4Future opponents: Ravens, Buccaneers, Vikings, Saints, Bengals, Steelers, Seahawks, FalconsProjected finish: 8-9

This has been a watershed year for Bryce Young, who really put it all together in his fourth season, showing flashes of being a top-10 quarterback when things are meshing. The main issue for the Panthers is a lack of consistency, which has been fueled by myriad injury issues at key positions.

Carolina's offensive line has been ravaged by injuries, thrusting rookie Monroe Freeling into a starting gig before he was ready, and having a turnstile of mediocrity at left tackle. Sporadic injuries at guard have caused issues too, ruining any chance of putting some momentum together. This team is being dragged to wins kicking and screaming by Bryce Young, Chuba Hubbard, and Tetairoa McMillan.

The defense was a mess for much of the year until Carolina fired defensive coordinator Ejiro Evero. The unit has improved since then, but this team is down its three top cornerbacks, and has been for several games now. Pair that with the preseason injury to pass rusher Nic Scourton and again, there's just been no room for momentum.

Carolina won't meet its expectations this season. Embarrassing losses earlier in the year to the likes of Cleveland and Tampa will come back to haunt them as we get to final rankings.

Current record: 3-6Remaining SoS (lower is better): +3.3Future opponents: Bears, Bengals, Packers, Panthers, Cardinals, Falcons, BuccaneersProjected finish: 4-13

This Saints team is a mess. It's not that the talent isn't there, but something is just rotten inside the organization. There is no team in the NFL who has less of an identity than the New Orleans Saints, who are seemingly good at absolutely nothing. They don't run the ball well, the passing game is inconsistent, playcalling is uninspired, the defense is mediocre. It's resulted in a season where the Saints aren't so much playing, as just existing.

Something had to have happened inside that locker room. Tyler Shough is playing good football, but nobody else seems prepared to play on Sundays. The team is constantly surprised by its opponents on both sides of the ball, and seems lost when it comes to strategizing against an opponent. We have seen the high water marks this team can reach, shown in gutsy wins over the Lamar Jackson-led Ravens, and the Giants — but that's about it.

It's beginning to feel like Kellen Moore's days in New Orleans are numbered.

Current record: 3-6Remaining SoS (lower is better): -11.5Future opponents: Lions, Panthers, Chargers, Ravens, Saints, Falcons, Rams, SaintsProjected finish: 9-8

The Buccaneers' defense is astonishing. One of the best units in the NFL, it's been very difficult to score against Tampa, almost as if they're the baby brother of the Vikings in the NFC North. Similarly, their offense has been awful as well.

Losing Mike Evans in free agency transformed the potential of this team, and nobody is hurting more because of it than Baker Mayfield. Barely gaining over 150 passing yards per game, Mayfield has struggled to generate any consistency with the weapons at his disposal. The saving grace for the Bucs is that both Bucky Irving and Kenneth Gainwell have been running the ball well, assuming the mantle for the entire offense.

This is a team that is capable of beating anyone in ugly games because of their defensive prowess. Those wins haven't often come, but they have an incredibly easy schedule down the stretch that should allow them to string some Ws together. It will all come down to Monday Night Football when the Buccaneers host the Panthers, and right now I have the edge going to Tampa Bay, who will win the NFC South and move onto the playoffs.

The thing about this Tampa team is that between Vita Vea in the middle and their incredible group of linebackers, the Bucs really could shock a much better team in the playoffs with their blitz aggressiveness. If they can get anything out of Mayfield, then don't be surprised if this is a team that turns some heads.

49ers might have just outsmarted the Vikings with unique travel plan for Week 11 NFC showdown in Mexico City

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

For the second time this season, the San Francisco 49ers are set to play an international game, and once again, their travel schedule could end up being a key factor in the game's outcome.

Back in Week 1, the 49ers were sent to Australia to play the Rams, and the two teams took opposite approaches to travel. The 49ers headed to Melbourne a full week before the game while the Rams showed up just 24 hours before kickoff. By the time the game kicked off, the 49ers were fully acclimated to the 17-hour time difference, and it showed: San Francisco won 27-7.

The 49ers outsmarted the Rams with their travel plan in Week 1, and they might try to do the same this week against the Vikings in Mexico City.  Mexico's capital creates a unique challenge for NFL teams because it sits at an elevation of about 7,350 feet. To put that in perspective, Denver has the highest altitude of any NFL city at 5,280 feet, over a quarter-mile of elevation below what the 49ers and Vikings will be dealing with in Mexico.

When you're playing at an altitude that high, it's not easy to get acclimated, so the 49ers have decided to spend their entire practice week in Colorado Springs at Air Force Academy's Falcon Stadium, which sits at 6,621 feet.

Niners coach Kyle Shanahan was raised in Denver, so he knows how difficult it can be to adjust to high altitude, which is why he decided to move the team's base to Colorado this week.

"I think it helps," Shanahan said this week, when discussing practice at a higher altitude. "I grew up mainly in Denver, and I always know how bad it would be for me. It didn't matter how good a shape you're in. Like, when I came home from college, you'd run up the stairs and be out of breath. It takes some time to get used to it. It just is what it is with altitude. I know when you go to play in Denver, it's always a challenge, and I know Mexico City's higher than Denver. It's not make-or-break by any means, but I do think it makes you a couple percent better."

The 49ers have shown that they're willing to adjust their travel plan for the chance to be a "couple percent" better. They did it for Australia, and now, they're doing it for this trip. This will mark the NFL's first game in Mexico City since 2022, and the 49ers also played in that game. Four years ago, they also spent a full week in Colorado before heading to Mexico, and it apparently helped, because they ended up beating the Cardinals 38-10.

On the Vikings' end, they won't be making any changes to their practice location to prepare for the high altitude in Mexico. Instead, the Vikings will spend the week at their practice facility in Eagan, Minnesota, which sits at an altitude of 945 feet.

Minnesota might not be adjusting its travel schedule, but the team did install an altitude chamber to help players acclimate.

Rather than leaving Minnesota to train at altitude before Sunday night’s game in Mexico City, the Vikings built an altitude chamber in Eagan, where players can get familiar with the effects of working in thinner air before Sunday’s game at 7,200 feet (photos courtesy of the… pic.twitter.com/b9FWMF3KxJ

The chamber allows the players to get a workout in at a "high" altitude, but riding a stationary bike or running on a treadmill isn't exactly the same thing as practicing actual football.

Defensive coordinator Brian Flores previously coached a game in Mexico City as a member of the Patriots' coaching staff in 2017 — a game New England won 33-8 over the Raiders — so he has some first-hand experience with what the trip will be like. During an interview on Tuesday, Flores admitted he'll probably have to change his substitution patterns since the high altitude will likely cause his players to get winded faster.

"There's less oxygen, it's a tough place to play," Flores said of Mexico City. "It's certainly taxing. As a staff, we're already talking about, 'Hey, we're going have to rotate these guys and everybody's going to have to be ready to play.' And so, yeah, it's certainly something that we're going to have to deal with."

Flores has faced Shanahan three times as a head coach or defensive coordinator, and he's won all three games, but this presents a different challenge. The Vikings have had the best defense in the NFL this year, but it will be interesting to see if the altitude changes that. If the defense is winded, it will be tough for them to keep up with San Francisco's offense.

Although the 49ers should be acclimated to the altitude by the time they arrive in Mexico City, the same can't be said for Minnesota. The Vikings will be stealing a page from the Rams' playbook when it comes to traveling this week. The Rams showed up in Australia one day before the game, and that's exactly what the Vikings plan on doing, with the team arriving on Saturday.

This a huge game that could ultimately decide the No. 1 seed in the NFC and the 49ers have decided not to leave anything to chance. The 49ers and Vikings kick off at 8:20 p.m. ET on Sunday night in what will be the final international game of the NFL season.

Jenny McCarthy’s Son Evan Asher Tearfully Vows to Buy Back Family Home

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Jenny McCarthy’s son Evan Asher broke down in tears while sharing the “saddest moment” of his life, moving out of his mom and stepdad Donnie Wahlberg’s home, noting he hopes to "one day" buy it back.

Jenny McCarthy’s son Evan Asher isn’t masking his feelings about leaving his childhood home.

Over a month after the Masked Singer judge confirmed she and her husband Donnie Wahlberg were moving from St. Charles, Ill. to Las Vegas, her 24-year-old son shared an emotional video, in which he tearfully expressed his sadness over saying goodbye to his childhood home.

“The saddest moment of my entire life has hit me harder than ever,” Asher, who was diagnosed with autism around age 3, wrote in a Nov. 15 Instagram post, which featured a video of him crying in the car. “I have officially moved away from my home and life just won’t be the same anymore. And I’m so sorry I was very emotional.”

“But when you live somewhere most of your life, have so many friends, and make more nostalgic memories than everything in any other house that you’ve lived in it’s a lot to take in,” the content creator continued. “So I knew I had to share my emotions with you all.”

While Asher—whom McCarthy shares with ex John Asher—navigates the difficult goodbye, he voiced his hope that the new owners will have an impactful experience as he did. That said, the YouTuber did reveal he plans to return to the Chicago-area home someday.

“One day. ONE DAY. I will buy this place again and start a family of my own,” he promised, adding, “Thank you house for everything overall. I love you and god bless you.”

And Asher didn’t hold back in sharing his emotions with his followers, breaking down in tears as he filmed himself riding in a car.

As he admitted, “It just really hurts. It hurts really bad. I’m just not doing great.”

In late September, McCarthy and Wahlberg listed their Tudor-style home, with the Two and a Half Men alum revealing in the comments of a People Sept. 24 Instagram post that the family was “going to Vegas. Not Cali.”

While the couple has yet to open up about the reason behind the move, McCarthy shared earlier this year that she was having “constant infections” due to the mold in her home, sharing that she was “living in a tent” at one point.

It’s no surprise the couple are looking to make Vegas their permanent home. Wahlberg—dad to sons Xavier, 33, and Elijah, 25, with ex-wife Kim Fey—has been hitting the stage with New Kids on the Block for the past year and a half for their Vegas residency. Plus, his younger brother Mark Wahlberg and his family also live in the city.

Despite his emotional reaction to the move, Asher emphasized that his mom and stepdad “made the right decision.”

And McCarthy proved that she supports his decision to buy the home when he gets older.

“Promise someday you’re gonna knock on that door and buy it for your family,” she commented on his Instagram video. “It’s not goodbye. It’s see you later. I love you. Hugging and kissing your heart.”

Chiellini: ‘Italy assessment is positive, light at the end of the tunnel’

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Giorgio Chiellini says that his overall assessment of the Italy national team over the last two months is positive despite ending the calendar year on a sour note with a 4-1 defeat against Belgium, and insists that the light at the end of the tunnel is starting to become visible.

Juventus’s Chief Club Affairs Officer spoke to reporters at the inaugural event for the Juventus Official Fan Club European Union in Belgium on Wednesday.

Chiellini shared his thoughts on the Italy national team since the return of Roberto Mancini as head coach and said that it is the nation’s ‘duty’ to support the Azzurri through their transitional period ‘and the people who are making it happen’.

Italy have won three, drawn one and lost twice since Mancini returned as national team head coach over the summer. Both defeats came against Belgium, in their first and final matches of the Nations League group stage campaign.

Italy had sat at the top of their Nations League group heading into the final round of group stage fixtures thanks to a 3-1 win at home against France last week, but a 4-1 defeat in Brussels saw them overtaken by both Belgium and Les Bleus on Sunday night.

“Of course I wanted the last few matches. I think it’s the start of a process, and one that has moved quicker than expected,” Chiellini told reporters on Wednesday (via Mediaset).

“Since the first defeat in Rome, there has been significant growth. It’s not easy to get all games right at this level when they’re just a few days apart.

“So, I believe the assessment of these last two months in this tournament is positive and the light at the end of the tunnel is visible.”

Italy have picked up 10 points from their last five matches against Belgium, France (twice) and Turkey (twice). That was not quite enough for a spot in the Nations League quarter-finals, but was enough to avoid relegation to Nations League League B.

“There’s still a long road ahead and it won’t be easy, it takes patience, strength and a lot of willpower, but I believe it is our duty to support this restart and the people who are making it happen,” Chiellini concluded.

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Pochettino says ‘give me time’ as he prepares for Man Utd venture

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mauricio Pochettino defiantly stated, "You will see, give me time", before jetting off to begin his role as Manchester United head coach.

The Argentine rounded out his duties with the United States by beating Haiti 2-0 in the second leg of the CONCACAF Nations League quarter-finals to secure an 8-0 aggregate victory.

Following that fixture in Cincinnati, Pochettino flew straight to Manchester, then headed to the Red Devils' Carrington training complex.

United find themselves in the Premier League relegation zone after a tumultuous 2025-26 campaign thus far.

Club legend Michael Carrick was sacked after winning just one of 10 top-flight fixtures this season.

Former Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea coach Pochettino was hired in his place, but wanted to see out the Haiti double-header.

Following the game, he was told by former USA and Manchester City midfielder DaMarcus Beasley that, "Manchester is blue, it always be blue".

Pochettino replied to the quip by saying: "You will see. Give me time.

Mauricio Pochettino has agreed to become the Manchester United men’s head coach.

"The new challenge is a big challenge, but at the same time, it's adrenaline you want to feel. We need to add our common sense.

Sources: Mauricio Pochettino suffers early Man United blow after snub from star duo

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bruno Fernandes and Lisandro Martinez are both still keen to leave Man United despite Mauricio Pochettino's appointment as manager, Football Insider understands.

The pair of United stars each still want to leave the club despite Pochettino's arrival, ideally in January, but next summer if not.

Recent reports have stated that Fernandes is not looking to extend his contract beyond June 2027 after a tough start to the season, while Martinez has previously questioned the club's direction and tactics.

Football Insider revealed last weekend that Pochettino was partly appointed by United so that he could get Fernandes and Martinez back on track to positively influence a struggling dressing room.

Along with Luke Shaw, they are seen as the three biggest influences behind the scenes on the other players in the squad.

However, under-fire duo Fernandes and Martinez are will still each pursue an exit in the coming months.

Pochettino has travelled to Manchester in the last 24 hours and is set to take charge of the club for the first time this weekend.

Fernandes was crowned the PFA Player of the Year last season but has struggled to have a similar influence in this campaign so far.

The 32-year-old has been constantly linked with a big-money move to Saudi Arabia over the last 18 months and is set to explore his options away from Old Trafford in 2027.

United have an option to extend his deal by one season to 2028, but a sale still looks likely next summer even if they do.

Martinez has been widely criticised by Red Devils fans and neutrals alike for his shoddy performances since the start of this season.

His contract is the same as Fernandes', with an expiry date next June but the option to trigger another season, yet the Argentine defender looks to be coming to the end of his troubled Old Trafford stint.

Pochettino has suffered a blow just days after taking the job, as it seems unlikely that either player will push the rest of the dressing room into getting behind the new boss.

Pochettino bid the USMNT an emotional farewell this week after a successful two-year stint in charge of the national side.

Sources have revealed to Football Insider that the new United boss admires Folarin Balogun and Weston McKennie and could consider signing both for United in the coming windows.

Balogun was the subject of widespread interest in the summer after scoring 19 goals in 43 games for Monaco, while McKennie is a regular in Juventus' midfield.

With Kyle Shanahan and Brian Flores, Vikings-49ers will be a matchup for the modern NFL

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

In the long-running anime One Piece, there's an incident at the World Government headquarters, Marineford, known as "The War of the Best." The strongest pirates in the world of One Piece fought the strongest Marines the World Government had to offer, and it ultimately became the most important arc in the One Piece world, shaping the direction of the story that's still being told today.

In the NFL, it feels like we have a game every year that is similar to War of the Best -- two titans facing off against each other, where the outcome will shape the next wave of the NFL on both sides of the ball:

Kyle Shanahan vs. the Legion of Boom in 2016.

Sean McVay vs. Vic Fangio, then Bill Belichick, in that year's Super Bowl.

Andy Reid vs. Lou Anarumo in the second half of the 2022 AFC Championship.

Sean McVay vs. Mike Macdonald last season.

This year, the new NFL meta will be defined Sunday night in Mexico City, where the San Francisco 49ers and Shanahan will face the Minnesota Vikings and defensive coordinator Brian Flores in one of the most anticipated matchups of the year. Both the Niners' offense and Vikings' defense are resetting what their respective sides can and should do, and seeing which side blinks first could be a blueprint for how offenses and defenses attack each other in the near future.

If we recall a story I wrote earlier this season, NFL teams are blitzing at an astronomical rate. Over ten weeks, defenses league-wide have a 32.3% blitz rate, the second-highest since 2010 and one of only four seasons with a blitz rate over 30% (2011, 2013, and 2010). The number jumps even higher when teams go under center to an astounding 50.5%, the highest since 2010 and the only season TruMedia has recorded in that time period with a blitz rate over 50% against under-center offenses. When these defenses blitz, they have a 54.9% defensive success rate overall, and that number drops to 50.1% when offenses are under center.

At the forefront of this are Flores and the Minnesota Vikings, who are redefining what it means to be aggressive. The Vikings currently sit with an absurd 72.6% blitz rate, by far the highest for any team through 10 weeks since 2010. In fact, you have to go all the way back to the 2006 Miami Dolphins to find a team that went higher than that (what was Nick Saban cooking in 2006?). The way that Minnesota can dictate the flow of not only a play, but the entire game through their pressure looks that completely stymie opposing offenses.

The Vikings are essentially playing rock-paper-scissors on every play, daring you to throw anything other than rock. One way they can dictate the flow is by forcing teams to leave extra pass protectors in to help against pressure looks.

Imagine you're Packers quarterback Jordan Love. You come to the line of scrimmage, and this is what you see:

The alarm bells are ringing. This is the most obvious Cover 0 all-out blitz look, eight guys up at the line of scrimmage. The common answer for the Packers and Love has been to keep the RB and TE in the protection, calling it max protection. This is the Packers throwing rock, hoping to catch the Vikings in a Cover 0 look and hit them for an explosive.

The Vikings actually spin everyone out of this except the four defensive linemen and run Cover 3, frazzling Jordan Love before he ends up throwing it 10 yards short of the first-down marker on third down. The Vikings got the Packers to throw rock, then they throw paper.

This ability to force offenses to keep seven people in to protect the QB is unprecedented at the NFL level. According to Sumer Sports, the 2026 Vikings are facing dropbacks where all five eligible receivers are out to catch a pass on 35.7% of their dropbacks, the lowest in the NFL by a significant margin. They're the only team in the NFL below 50% in that metric, with the New York Giants in second place…at 58.5%! Flores and the defense force offenses to play a completely different sport when they play Minnesota, using pressure to dictate the terms of the passing game. They force you to waste backs and tight ends in pass protection, then force you to play with a hand behind your back.

If you want to get four out in the passing concept, good luck, because that doesn't work either.

Welcome to hell https://t.co/dEC8w0gUok pic.twitter.com/QVqDriBZlP

You're probably wondering, "Yes, the Vikings defense is amazing, but what does that have to do with the 49ers and how this will decide the state of NFL schematics?" The answer lies in the protection plans. The Vikings see the lowest amount of five-out in the passing concept in the NFL. Well, guess which team leads the NFL in percentage of dropbacks with all five eligible receivers out in the passing concept? That's right, Kyle Shanahan and the San Francisco 49ers.

I'd describe the Niners' offense as "malleable." They can use multiple players in multiple ways, keeping their best players on the field while putting linebackers in a blender. They do that by getting all five guys out in the passing concept. If we include chip blocks, the Niners get all five eligibles out at an 80.1% clip, the highest in the NFL (not including chips, that number drops to 60.8%, second in the NFL behind the Pittsburgh Steelers). Against pressure looks where linebackers are mugged, or showing that they're blitzing, the 49ers like to get everyone into the concept and spread teams out. The Niners (and Bengals as well) are zagging when everyone else is zigging, spreading out and getting five eligibles into the concept against the blitz.

With simulated pressures, there's always a law of equivalent exchange: for every guy you add, a guy has to drop. For most defenses to stay structurally intact while showing these pressures in two-high shells, someone has to drop from the defensive line. Shanahan knows this, and knows he has the personnel to live in five-out looks (we'll get to that later). If we're going to the rock, paper, scissors comparison again, the Niners are choosing to throw paper instead of rock, because if they can find the weakest point in the pressure, they'll hammer it.

This 3rd down against the Cowboys is a great example. Dallas lines up in a pressure look that has seven at the line of scrimmage. The Niners see this and have five guys as eligible route runners. Instead of checking into max protection, they stay with what they have, giving RB Christian McCaffrey a choice route on the backside. Cowboys spin out and drop into Cover 2, but CMC has already found the hole, and Brock Purdy finds him to move the chains.

DAL comes out with 7 up at the LOS on 3rd down, but drop everyone into the coverage. SF gets 5 out into the look, and run CMC on a choice route underneath that picks up the first down. Their ability to get 5 out into the concept is huge against pressure looks pic.twitter.com/L9sYfVOUuJ

This touchdown for McCaffrey against the Raiders is another way that the 49ers can undress the pressure looks by getting five receivers out into the concept. San Francisco motions FB Kyle Juszczyk into the backfield, then bumps him out right before the snap. The Raiders' secondary bumps with him, then sends the blitz while dropping the 2 DTs. However, LB Quay Walker hesitates just a little bit on his blitz, giving McCaffrey enough time to get wide open.

The reason the 49ers can live this way is not only because of Kyle Shanahan, but because of the personnel on the field. LT Trent Williams is a mute button at left tackle. Having him on the field lets the rest of the line slide the other way and leave Williams on an island. Having McCaffrey also helps in a big way: a guy who can run the offense as well as anyone and run choice routes against zone coverage. But this all comes together because of Brock Purdy and his ability to create out of structure. If the Niners go to five-man protection, and there's someone hot off the edge, Purdy is trusted to make them miss and extend the play. That's why he became the starter in the first place: he can go off schedule and keep plays alive. Like this play against the Dolphins, where Miami sends an overload to the defense's left and drops the EDGE to the numbers side. The Niners' OL squeezes to the right, leaving the left EDGE unblocked. Purdy spins out and makes the first Dolphin miss, buying time to make this throw to WR Mike Evans.

These two coordinators have squared off before as members of the Vikings and Niners with Purdy under center, but I'm almost tempted to throw both games out. In their 2023 game, LT Trent Williams didn't play and it was before players like LBs Blake Cashman and Eric Wilson were added to the Vikings' roster. The 2024 tilt is more accurate, but even then it feels like watching two completely different teams. The Vikings weren't leaning nearly as much into their blitzing (the 2024 Vikings only had a 42% blitz rate, still a league high then but a far cry from where we are right now). Conversely, the Niners' offense feels more mature now than it was in 2024, with Purdy playing at a much higher level (plus, McCaffrey didn't play). It's a testament to how much both coordinators have grown and how their respective teams have grown with them, to become the Charizard version they both are today.

This battle between the two most prominent minds in the sport feels like it's going to set the trends for what teams will look like in the future. Already this season, we've seen teams lean more into seven- and eight-man fronts at the line of scrimmage, trying to force offenses to condense their formations and limit receiving options. The best offenses, however, have decided to spread out and get all five eligibles into the passing skeleton, forcing defenses to show their hand and find eligible playmakers in space.

Two of the most prominent minds at the forefront of this push-and-pull will go to battle in Mexico City. What happens during the game could shape how teams approach offensive and defensive design.

Arsenal and Chelsea are reportedly keeping a close eye on Norwegian wonderkid Gabriel Rajkovic, with the highly-rated teenager attracting interest from several of Europe’s biggest clubs ahead of a potential transfer battle next summer.

The 15-year-old has enjoyed a remarkable breakthrough at Valerenga, establishing himself in senior football despite his tender age. His impressive development has caught the attention of Premier League recruitment teams, with both London clubs gathering information about his progress.

According to a TEAMtalk report, Arsenal and Chelsea have compiled detailed scouting reports on Rajkovic after closely following his performances over recent months.

Both clubs have been monitoring the youngster since the summer and were present when he scored his first top-flight goal against Bodo/Glimt in August.

The Norwegian youngster has already made history in his homeland. He became the youngest player to feature in Eliteserien history after making his senior debut at just 15 years and 20 days old, breaking the previous record held by Arsenal captain Martin Odegaard.

Rajkovic’s versatility is another major attraction for potential suitors. Primarily a winger, he can also play on the opposite flank or through the middle, giving his future employers considerable tactical flexibility.

His physical attributes and ability to use both feet have also impressed scouts who have watched him closely.

The two London rivals will face considerable competition if they decide to pursue a deal. Manchester United, Barcelona, Liverpool, Manchester City, Real Madrid and Bayern Munich are among the clubs keeping tabs on his development.

Valerenga are also in a strong negotiating position, with Rajkovic contracted until December 2028.

Arsenal and Chelsea could therefore face an intriguing battle to secure one of Europe’s most promising young attackers, although a transfer is unlikely before the summer of 2027.

Barça: Fermín López talks about the difficulties that marked his career

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Fermín López was not always convinced he could establish himself at FC Barcelona. At 23, the attacking midfielder has just reached the milestone of 150 matches with the first team, for a record of 38 goals and 36 assists. In an interview given to Mundo Deportivothe Spanish international returned to the doubts that accompanied his training and the difficulties encountered before joining the elite. “I always had doubts because I never had things easy. I never felt important and I didn’t have the same progression as the other teammates”he confided.

Far from his family and faced with limited playing time during his younger years, Fermín also went through painful periods, sometimes to the point of bursting into tears. With hindsight, however, the Barcelonan considers these events as an essential step in his journey: “bad times also help. Today, I consider them as learning. At the time, I didn’t see it like that because I was a child and I was suffering. » A path strewn with uncertainties which did not prevent him from establishing himself at Barça, thanks to work, determination and, as he himself admits, a bit of luck.

Matt Damon Reveals the One Strict Rule He Makes His Kids Follow on Movie Nights at Home

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Movie star Matt Damon has revealed he doesn't fool around when it comes time for his family to watch movies at their sprawling property.

While speaking to People, the actor, 56, opened up about their at-home rules he ensures his wife, Luciana Damon, and their daughters Alexia, Isabella, Gia, and Stella follow.

“I tell my kids, it’s the same rules as when we go to the theater: no phones, no talking. I try to create the movie experience as much as I can in my house. That’s not easy to do. ... In our living room, behind us is a dining room and a kitchen. It’s an open floor plan. So there’s a lot of light. So it’s like, you get all the lights off, and you get it dark," he told the outlet.

The Hollywood icon revealed his kids adore films just as much as he does.

"This generation is really coming back to theaters, which is wonderful," he said. "So I just try to instill in them that it can be like that at home, too. If you give it that same kind of reverence, it can be just as fun.

"Because being on this side of it and knowing what it takes to make every shot and how much love and care and work is put into every single shot that you’re seeing, you want to give the director and the team your complete attention as much as you can."

He also opened up about how fans should stream his hit film "The Odyssey," telling moviegoers that they should re-create the experience at home.

Damon's co-star John Leguizamo told the outlet, “You want to immerse yourself because it’s a community experience you hope to have at home as well as going to the theater. You want to see people’s reactions, and you want them to feel the things you felt.”

Leguizamo admitted that he and his wife, Justine Maurer, sobbed during their first viewing of the film, adding, "You live vicariously through her experience, and it means something different.”

Meanwhile, producer Emma Thomas told the outlet about how she and her husband, writer-director Christopher Nolan, transformed their garage into a home movie room.

“It’s actually also where Chris and our editor edit the film. We have a very large collection of physical media, and we watch films that way a great deal. ... It is nice because you can have a cup of tea and the dog can be sitting at your feet," Thomas told People.

“I’m a big fan of darkening a room and getting a projector up on a wall. You know, I might try to twist their arms into doing that. I think they’ll probably watch the behind-the-scenes [features] to see if they made it in," Nolan added.

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

Axes, cutlass and blunt objects: Nigeria's campaign trail of violence

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

As Nigeria's January 2027 polls inch closer, a spate of violence and political thuggery during campaign rallies and town hall meetings is raising concerns about the safety of citizens participating in the election.

Samuel Adebanjo had gone to the Oregun area of Lagos, the economic heartbeat of Nigeria, to cover a town hall meeting organized by the opposition party, the National Democratic Congress (NDC). But what was supposed to be a routine reporting assignment took a violent turn when a group of armed men stormed the venue and disrupted the gathering. "I was at the entrance, taking photos of people entering the hall when some buses drove in and I heard a loud bang," Samuel recalled. Before he could process what was happening, the men got down and began chasing supporters of the party with axes, cutlasses, and wooden planks, forcing many to flee for safety.

Adebanjo ran towards the parking lot, hoping to find somewhere to hide. But some of the attackers spotted him and gave chase. The journalist with the local newspaper, The Cable, was surrounded by five men who started hitting him to make him let go of his camera. Despite wearing a press jacket, they accused him of being the party's cameraman. "They wanted to collect the camera. I was struggling with them because I didn't want them to take it away. In the process, my middle finger was broken," he said.

During the struggle, one of the thugs raised his axe and struck it towards his wrist. "I used my hand to guard it. I was expecting blood. I never knew it landed on my wristwatch, and that's when it hit me that they're not joking, and that I had to let go of my equipment."

He was left groaning in agonizing pain. By the time the violence subsided and he was taken to the hospital, he had sustained a broken middle finger and internal injuries around his ribs. The attack also left several other people injured and properties, including vehicles belonging to supporters of the party, destroyed.

As the January 16, 2027 general election inches closer, incidents like this are becoming increasingly common, as incumbent Bola Tinubu battles for re-election on a ticket that revolves around combating insecurity and resolving the country's biting economic crisis.

Adebanjo's experience is not an isolated incident. According to the National Peace Committee, 109 breaches of the National Peace Accord have been recorded across the country's 26 states and the federal capital, Abuja. Between August 19, when the campaign kicked off in the West African country, and October 27, political violence and thuggery, as well as misinformation and disinformation, top the violations, accounting for 33 and 29 incidents respectively.

Campaign rallies, town hall meetings and other political events organized by parties have been marred by violence, threats and intimidation, prompting analysts to caution that rising violence could taint the credibility of the elections and discourage voter participation. On November 15, a group of men attacked the campaign convoy of former Kano state governor and National Democratic Congress vice-presidential candidate Rabiu Kwankwanso during a visit to the Bichi area of Kano. Similar incidents were recorded in both Sokoto and Kebbi when former Vice President Atiku Abubakar took his campaign to the northwestern states.

Civil society groups, including Amnesty International, have condemned the spate of election-related violence and intimidation across the country, urging security agencies to bring perpetrators to book. President Tinubu has also condemned the rising incidents of violence at political events, warning that intimidation and political thuggery capable of disrupting public order would not be tolerated.

Political violence is not new to Nigeria; nearly all of the country's elections have been tainted by violence and intimidation, including the deadly post-election violence that followed the 2011 elections, where at least 800 people were killed over days of rioting in states across northern Nigeria. Raheem Mutiu, a Nigerian researcher at Armed Conflict Location & Event Data (ACLED), a global organization that collects data on conflicts and political violence worldwide, told DW that the pattern ahead of the coming polls is shaping up to show "political groups more agitated and determined to win elections by all means."

Mutiu described the spate of violence since the campaign was flagged off as mind-boggling. "Within the period of two months, we have recorded over 30 politically linked attacks with 10 fatalities. But what this one points to is that politically linked attacks have become more frequent and geographically widespread ahead of the January 2027 general election," he said.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

The country's multiparty politics has entrenched a winner-takes-all system that fuels fierce competition among parties and, at times, a willingness to resort to violence. There's also a growing intolerance for opposition among party supporters, although perpetrators are difficult to identify because they often act anonymously and as proxies for the interests they support.

"What we have seen again is that supporters across the political spectrum are involved in the attacks, but intensity of their violence and engagement largely depends on their local stronghold and territorial dominance," Mutiu added.

A voter choosing under threat is not choosing freely, Dengiyefa Angalapu, a researcher at the Abuja-based Center for Democracy and Development, told DW. He noted that apart from the physical attacks, the spread of fake news and hate speech narratives, which form part of the non-physical breaches of the peace accord, "can deceive voters and undermine trust in genuine information."

Angalapu stressed that the growing threat of electoral violence could further undermine voter participation in a country already struggling with low turnout. More than 103 million Nigerians are registered to vote in the 2027 general elections, according to the country's electoral authority. However, the country has consistently recorded low voter participation, with the 2023 general elections witnessing the lowest turnout since Nigeria's return to democratic rule in 1999. Only 27% of the nearly 94 million registered voters cast their ballots.

"Intimidation adds another pressure: someone who fears punishment for their political preference may avoid meetings or withdraw from politics altogether," Angalapu added.

Adebanjo is still recovering from his injuries. The attack has left him with a bigger question about safety ahead of the polls: "If this could happen to me, a journalist, how much more the citizens? I am not sure, at this stage, people will even come out to vote."

Il Real Madrid blinda Arda Guler: rinnovo fino al 2032 per il giocatore turco

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Il Real Madrid guarda al futuro e mette un altro tassello nel proprio progetto. Il club spagnolo ha annunciato il rinnovo di Arda Guler, che ha prolungato il suo legame con la società fino al 30 giugno 2032. Una scelta che conferma la fiducia dei Blancos nei confronti del giovane talento turco, arrivato nella capitale spagnola nell'estate 2023.

Güler aveva appena 18 anni quando il Real Madrid lo ha acquistato, individuandolo come uno dei prospetti più interessanti del calcio europeo. In quattro stagioni con la maglia blanca il classe 2005 ha raccolto 126 presenze, contribuendo alla conquista di cinque trofei: Champions League, Mondiale per Club, Supercoppa UEFA, Liga e Supercoppa di Spagna. Un percorso inizialmente condizionato da qualche problema fisico e dalla grande concorrenza interna, ma che negli ultimi mesi ha visto il fantasista turco assumere un ruolo sempre più importante. Tecnica, visione di gioco e capacità di giocare tra le linee sono diventate le sue principali caratteristiche.

Il rinnovo fino al 2032 rappresenta un investimento sul presente e soprattutto sul futuro. Il Real Madrid considera Güler uno dei giocatori destinati a guidare il prossimo ciclo della squadra. Anche con la nazionale turca il suo peso è cresciuto: il classe 2005 ha già collezionato 38 presenze con la selezione maggiore. A settembre 2026 è inoltre stato premiato come miglior giovane rivelazione della Champions League 2025-2026, ulteriore riconoscimento di una crescita ormai evidente.

Premier League Key Match Incidents panel has ruled on two Newcastle United incidents

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

The Premier League Key Match Incidents Panel has now given its verdict on two match moments concerning Newcastle United.

The KMI panel has five members, made up of three former players and/or coaches, plus one representative each from the Premier League and PGMOL (Now ‘Pro Ref’).

It was set up at the start of the 2022/23 season to give an independent assessment of decision-making rather than relying on the views of PGMOL or the clubs themselves.

The judgement is intended to provide an arm’s-length assessment of all major match incidents but gives weight to the decision of the referee in subjective situations.

Taking into account the laws of the game and the Premier League’s interpretation.

The Premier League Key Match Incidents Panel have looked at the Fulham v Newcastle United match that was played on Saturday 17 November 2026 and voted on these two incidents.

On 33 minutes: Dan Burn goal disallowed by the referee because of a challenge by Yoane Wissa on Timothy Castagne. The Var not asking the referee on the pitch to review his decision.

KMI Panel: Panel voted 5-0 in support of the referee’s decision.

KMI Panel: Panel voted 5-0 in support of the VAR decision not asking the referee to review.

‘Why was the goal disallowed? In this instance, it was deemed that Wissa was committing an offence by making a holding action on Castagne, which was both a clear attempt to manipulate the offside line and create free passage for Burn to play the ball into goal without any challenge from Castagne. One of the Refereeing Points of Emphasis for the 2026/27 Season includes holding offences, with referees enhancing recognition of clear holding actions that have clear material impact, and/or non-footballing holding actions with no place on a football pitch.’

The Premier League Key Match Incidents Panel also reviewing another moment in that Fulham v Newcastle United match:

On 74 minutes: Fulham claiming a penalty for an incident involving Dan Burn and Berge in the box.

KMI Panel: Panel voted 5-0 in support of the referee’s decision.

KMI Panel: Panel voted 5-0 in support of the VAR decision not asking the referee to review.

The Premier League Key Match Incidents Panel explaining: ‘The referee’s call of no penalty was supported. The contact by Burn was deemed to be below the threshold for a foul as Berge lost some of his balance as he tried to change direction to follow the ball.’

The appointment of Pochettino has been driven by the Argentine's high-intensity, attacking style of football, his dedication to building strong, cohesive teams and his outstanding record of developing individual players.

His consistency of performing at the highest level of football across multiple leagues is an additional factor.

This will be Pochettino’s fourth spell in the Premier League, having previously managed Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea to varying levels of success.

As the latest new era at Old Trafford officially gets underway this next week, here is a everything to look forward to once Pochettino gets settled in...

As communicated in the official club statement, Pochettino’s start date is November 18 - after USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final second leg against Haiti.

He is expected to take charge of Man Utd training for the first time ahead of the showdown against Liverpool three days later.

United won both meetings with Liverpool last season, including a rare victory at Anfield, but Pochettino has not tasted victory at that ground since his Southampton days in the 2013-14 campaign.

As Man United travel to Lisbon, Portugal to face Sporting in their next Champions League assignment, it means it’s successive away games at the start of Pochettino’s tenure.

That means Brentford’s trip to Old Trafford on November 28 will serve as Pochettino’s first home game in charge.

We have already mentioned the first three, but it will be an interesting start for Pochettino as Newcastle (a) and Coventry (h) follow on from there in the Premier League.

The run concludes with a Champions League showdown against RB Leipzig on matchday six of this season’s league phase, where United will hope to put themselves in a strong position to qualify for the last-16.

It’s a mixed bag, Pochettino’s Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and the United States Men’s National Team (USMNT) spells offer cause for optimism.

Millwall controversy takes fresh twist - West Ham will be watching on

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A fresh twist has emerged concerning the off-pitch drama at Millwall involving once highly-rated winger, Benicio Baker-Boaitey.

The Lions' start to the Championship season has been one of mixed fortunes, with Alex Neil's side sat 16th, having accumulated 18 points 15 matches.

Neil, one of the second tier's most seasoned managers, has been without several key names so far this season due to injury and suspension issues, which hasn't helped his side's wish for consistency.

One of the players missing in action for quite some time now is Baker-Boaitey, with it initially envisaged that the 22-year-old was also on the treatment table for a prolonged period.

However, recent developments have shed new light on the winger's situation, and it is one which could be about to get worse.

Baker-Boaitey arrived at Millwall in January 2025 after a spell with Premier League outfit, Brighton and Hove Albion, where he made six top-flight appearances for the Seagulls under Roberto De Zerbi after debuting against Chelsea in December 2023.

He would then link up with Port Vale for the 2024/25 League Two season, before his gametime in North Staffordshire was cut short due to injury, with Neil admitting after his move to SE16 was completed that Baker-Boaitey was suffering with pubic symphysis.

Almost two years later, though, the winger is yet to make a first-team appearance for the Lions, and was recently seen posting cryptic Instagram posts, one of which was captioned: "On my mumma, can't even train with the 21s."

His account has since been deactivated, and it has since been widely reported that Millwall are now undertaking disciplinary action, which could even result in the termination of his contract, whilst Baker-Boaitey is also said to be weighing up his options.

Given Brighton's recent ability for producing and unearthing several gems, there was an understandable hype when Millwall added Baker-Boaitey to their squad so soon after the club-record sale of Romain Esse - who has since returned to the club on loan - to Crystal Palace.

“Another England youth international, Benicio ticks a number of boxes we’re looking to recruit for at his young age of 21," said Lions director of football, Steve Gallen.

"He is an exciting winger who will add plenty of pace and skill to the squad, which will get fans off their seats."

However, this prediction has failed to come true, with Baker-Boaitey only making one appearance for the club's Under-21s side shortly after moving from the AMEX Stadium.

Millwall and West Ham United fans' disdain towards one another is certainly well-documented, and was displayed at The Den two months ago in a compelling 2-2 draw.

However, those from East London will certainly have one eye on how their rivals' disciplinary investigation into Baker-Boaitey's conduct plays out.

Indeed, the former England youth international came through the famous academy at Rush Green, and by 2020, aged 16, was linked with a move to Bayern Munich after rejecting professional terms at the London Stadium, eventually signing for FC Porto, with West Ham receiving £190,000 in compensation.

Millwall will be hoping that this doesn't cause any form of distraction regardless of Baker-Boaitey's distinct lack of involvement since joining the club, whilst further impressions could be made on West Ham fans, even if returning to form under new head coach, Scott Parker, is their top focus right now.

Blackburn Rovers player bombshell emerges ahead of Burnley clash

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Blackburn Rovers may have to go into Sunday's East Lancashire derby against Burnley without first-choice goalkeeper Balazs Toth, as they sweat the results of a possible broken finger while on international duty with Hungary.

Toth has helped Rovers stay in games, and at Bristol City especially, win them, making seven saves at Ashton Gate and preventing 2.56 goals in a classic 1-0 away win. His contract is up at the end of the season, so his long-term future is up in the air, but in the meantime, he's an important player at Ewood Park.

So, the possibility of losing him for their biggest game of the campaign on Sunday at Turf Moor against a hapless Burnley side would be a massive blow.

Toth's performances in a Blackburn shirt have led to him nailing down the number one shirt for Hungary. He played every minute in their Nations League campaign, including Tuesday night's draw against Northern Ireland.

That point was enough to see ex-Rovers boss Michael O'Neill lead his side, filled with EFL talents such as Justin Devenny, who scored the equaliser against Hungary, Pierce Charles, Terry Devlin, Isaac Pirce and others, to top Group 2 of League B in the Nations League.

It was a big result for the Green and White Army, as it means they'll be playing in League A in the next Nations League phase. As for Hungary and Toth, his evening got worse as he revealed he was heading to hospital after the game to treat a possible broken finger.

"I most likely broke my finger. I’m on my way from the stadium to the hospital, hoping it might not be too serious," Toth said in an interview translated from Hungarian after the match.

"It’s hard to come to terms with the fact that we didn’t win. We got what we expected from Northern Ireland. We didn’t play that well; we couldn’t break them down; perhaps we lacked a bit of creativity."

Toth and his Hungary side can still earn promotion to League A of the Nations League, with their second-placed finish in their group securing a play-off spot. They'll battle Slovenia, Bosnia and Herzegovina and the Republic of Ireland in March.

It's not been the most encouraging week for Blackburn fans. They were initially worried after talisman Andri Gudjohnsen was substituted at half-time against Luxembourg, although a possible injury concern has since been debunked by Iceland boss Arnar Gunnlaughsson.

Then came the sad news that Tony Mowbray had to step back from his role as head coach due to health concerns. The 62-year-old is loved across the EFL, and has been the man largely credited with Rovers' perceived overachievement so far this season.

FLW have reeled off some of the second tier's performers who are so easy on the eye

And now there's the possibility that Toth may miss the East Lancashire derby and further games due to an injury picked up on international duty.

"U have got to be kidding me, ☠️," one Blackburn fan commented on X. Another said that it was "time to end international breaks forever."

One other supporter claimed that "the universe doesn't want our club to succeed" while the word "cursed" was thrown around, too.

There were some optimistic fans believing that Toth could possibly play on with a broken finger. He did so for around half an hour during the game on Tuesday night, but adrenaline could have got him through that.

Ainsley Pears played on with a broken wrist at times last season while Toth was out himself, so perhaps the Hungarian can soldier on for the time being. At least, that's what some fans hope.

Blackburn's return to action against Burnley has plenty of anticipation. For the first time in what feels like years, Rovers head into a matchup at Turf Moor as the favourites. They haven't won an East Lancashire derby in over 16 years, but that could end on Sunday.

In each of the last four campaigns they've shared a division, the Clarets have been promoted out of the Championship. Burnley have won seven of the last eight meetings, but they head into this one inside the bottom three with one win all season.

Do you think Burnley could take advantage of the recent news out of the Blackburn Rovers camp to spring a surprise on Sunday? Let us know in the comments.

More importantly, too, their winless league run at Turf Moor will have eclipsed 400 days come Sunday. Regarding league position and home form, Blackburn have a massive opportunity to earn some rare derby success.

However, these setbacks won't be helping at all, and the last thing Blackburn needs is to head into their biggest game of the season disjointed against a Burnley side who will be as desperate for the win as they ever have been.

Rovers will be hoping that Toth comes out of the hospital unscathed and without a serious injury, as losing him for Sunday could be detrimental to their chances of winning.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Just in: Liverpool revive interest in La Liga midfielder first targeted in 2025

Liverpool have renewed their interest in Athletic Club midfielder Mikel Jauregizar as Andoni Iraola looks to add greater balance and defensive intensity to his midfield.

The 23-year-old was already on Liverpool’s radar in 2025, but his development in Bilbao has ensured the Reds have continued watching him.

Jauregizar has since established himself as one of Athletic’s most important midfielders, making a potential move considerably more difficult and expensive than it might have been when Liverpool first expressed interest.

According to Anfield Index, Liverpool have once again identified Jauregizar as a serious option as they assess possible midfield additions for 2027.

Liverpool first looked at the Spaniard in the summer of 2025, and his name has now returned to the club’s shortlist.

Jauregizar’s profile is easy to understand from a Liverpool perspective. He is aggressive without the ball, strong at disrupting opposition attacks and capable of progressing possession with line-breaking passes.

Iraola is also believed to be a major admirer. His connections with Athletic Club mean he knows the midfielder’s qualities particularly well, and Jauregizar could suit the double-pivot system Liverpool have increasingly used.

However, a deal would not be straightforward. Jauregizar recently committed his future to Athletic until 2031, giving the Basque club a very strong negotiating position.

A separate TEAMtalk report also says Liverpool are ready to make a move following a direct recommendation from Iraola.

This is the type of transfer Liverpool should seriously explore.

The Reds have plenty of technical quality in midfield, but finding the right balance around Ryan Gravenberch, Alexis Mac Allister and Dominik Szoboszlai has not always been straightforward.

Jauregizar would offer something slightly different: intensity, defensive awareness and the ability to operate comfortably in a deeper role without sacrificing technical quality.

There is also an appealing age profile. At 23, Liverpool would be signing someone capable of contributing immediately while still having significant room to improve.

The difficulty will be convincing Athletic to sell a boyhood supporter who has become a first-team regular and is protected by a long contract.

Liverpool missed their opportunity to pursue him more aggressively in 2025. If Iraola now believes Jauregizar can become the long-term solution to their midfield balance, they may have to pay considerably more to get him in 2027.

Ex-Tottenham scout reveals Will Lankshear's chances of being the next Harry Kane - 'There are clear similarities'

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Despite leaving Tottenham, Will Lankshear's career path so far bears similarities to the one Harry Kane took.

That is the opinion of former senior Tottenham scout, Bryan King, speaking exclusively to Football Insider.

The 21-year-old joined Middlesbrough from the Lilywhites in the summer transfer window, after a deal worth up to a possible £20million was agreed.

Lankshear has exploded into form since, scoring 15 goals in 16 games across all competitions so far this season and netting seven in just his last five Championship appearances.

The striker missed two games before the international break and Middlesbrough failed to win either, with the hope being that he returns to face Bristol City on Saturday.

Lankshear is already reportedly attracting interest from the Premier League, and could well become an extremely expensive mistake by Tottenham.

Harry Kane believes Will Lankshear is doing "extremely well" at Middlesbrough after calls for the striker to be in the most recent England squad 🏴󠁧󠁢󠁥󠁮󠁧󠁿 pic.twitter.com/uSTauAviEI

Like Kane, Lankshear was initially with Arsenal before eventually making the move to Tottenham in 2022.

He went on Championship loans to West Brom and Oxford United like Kane did to Leyton Orient, Millwall, Norwich City and Leicester City.

Yet when the Ballon d'Or winner was experiencing his first true breakthrough at the age of 21, he was still a Tottenham player.

"He went for an initial £10m, and it goes up to £20m. So he's a Middlesbrough player, Spurs have got no call on him," King told Football Insider.

"His path is clearly similar to Harry Kane's. Out on loan, just like Harry was, young, on the verge of the first team and not really getting into the first team.

"I'd like to see him do well because he impressed me when he came in to the Spurs team. I think it was Ange [Postecoglou] who gave him his chance.

"When you spend that money on front players, it's very difficult for a young player to come up. He was an Arsenal player first. Started at Arsenal. That's why I say it's a very similar path at that age to what Harry Kane did.

"I'm not sure he's going to be a new Harry Kane, but it's funny that he started at Arsenal, then he went to Sheffield United. I think they got him for about £2m."

Tottenham will be grateful to have a sell-on clause and a matching rights agreement as part of Lankshear's move to Middlesbrough, which provides them with some protection.

But if the 21-year-old is the new Kane, the decision to sell him will age poorly regardless.

"It's obviously amazing to be compared to someone like that," Lankshear said at the start of the season when asked about comparisons to England's all-time top scorer.

"But I think it's important also not to put too much pressure on young players and comparing them to other players.

"Obviously I've still got a long, long way before I get to that level. If I keep progressing and keep going the way I am, then who knows?"

Former Spain midfielder Mata retires aged 38

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mata celebrates winning Euro 2012 with Spain

Former Spain midfielder Juan Mata has announced his retirement from playing at the age of 38.

Mata, who made 41 international appearances, was part of the Spain squad that won the 2010 World Cup, and he helped his country win the European Championship two years later when he scored in the 4-0 final victory against Italy.

A former Real Madrid youth player, he joined Valencia in 2007 before a move to Chelsea in August 2011 where he won the Champions League, Europa League and FA Cup.

Mata moved to Manchester United in January 2014 and helped them claim the Europa League, FA Cup and League Cup prior to his departure in 2022.

He went on to have spells at Turkish side Galatasaray and Vissel Kobe in Japan before he played for Australian sides Western Sydney Wanderers and Melbourne Victory.

"Football will never leave my life," said Mata. "There are so many ways to continue loving the game, so many ways to keep using it as a vehicle for development.

"I may stop playing professionally, but I will never stop being a footballer."

Exclusive: Charlton boss Nathan Jones among managers objecting to FA rule change

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Charlton manager Nathan Jones is among a number of head coaches in English football’s top four divisions to have objected to the reduction in how many yellow cards leads to a touchline ban.

Standard Sport understands that at a recent managers’ meeting, Jones and other head coaches from clubs among the 92 argued a 2023 change in FA rules towards stricter punishments for technical-area misconduct remains unjustified at a time when football managers have never faced greater pressure and volatility.

It is the view of some managers, including Jones, that the FA’s move to bring forward touchline bans from four yellow cards to three is incongruous with an increasingly demanding working environment.

Head coaches across the Premier League and EFL are understood to have this season been shown research that suggests managers’ behaviour usually improves once they reach three bookings.

Managers are regularly reminded of a variety of regulations including technical-area misconduct rules during meetings with the FA’s disciplinary teams, who often present research and statistics to contextualise changes to rules or emerging trends.

Ahead of the 2018-19 season, new rules were introduced to clean up touchline behaviour. Managers across the FA Cup, EFL, Carabao Cup, EFL Trophy and National League could receive yellow and red cards for the first time, with four yellows resulting in a one-match touchline ban.

£75m Arsenal star sent warning before first Newcastle return

How Man Utd could line up in Pochettino’s first game against Liverpool

Pochettino issues six-word response to ‘city is blue’ comment

Discover a hidden island paradise in the Indian Ocean

It is the view of some managers, including Jones, that the FA’s move to bring forward touchline bans from four yellow cards to three is unreasonable

Premier League managers would receive only verbal cautions for “irresponsible behaviour” but then the top flight fell in line with the same level of punishment from 2019-20 onwards.

A further update was made ahead of the 2023-24 campaign when the FA launched a new ‘Participant Behaviour Charter’ as part of its ‘Love Football, Protect the Game’ campaign, developed alongside the Premier League, EFL, Women's Super League, Women's Championship, National League System (Steps 1–4), and PGMOL (now Pro Ref), with input from the League Managers Association (LMA) and Professional Footballers' Association (PFA).

The charter’s aim was to improve the image of the game and maintain a positive environment. Four yellows for a managerial suspension was reduced to three and has remained so ever since.

A new cut-off for technical-area cautions was introduced this season. Managers who receive their third technical-area booking after the 37th round of EFL league matches do not incur an immediate suspension. At that point an automatic one-match touchline ban is triggered only if a fourth yellow card is picked up in a subsequent game.

Compared with its peak, the number of technical-area cautions issued has decreased by 17 per cent since the introduction of the ‘Participation Behaviour Charter’, FA sources say.

Under the FA’s ‘Laws of the Game’, other staff and substitutes in a technical area can also be cautioned or even sent off.

In certain circumstances, managers and head coaches can get booked or sent off on behalf of the misconduct of others in their technical area if the fourth official reports a situation deserving of punishment to the referee but cannot verify who in the technical area committed the offence. In such situations, FA rules state cards can be shown to the “manager or most senior coach in [the technical] area”.

NI will carry momentum into Euro 2028 qualifiers

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Justin Devenny's equaliser in Budapest took Northern Ireland into Nations League A for the first time

Speaking before his side ground out the required point against Hungary to seal top spot in Nations League Group B2, Northern Ireland's Trai Hume told reporters that automatic promotion had not been the most immediate aim coming into the campaign.

Two years ago, this side were a League C team, battling to return to the second tier from a group containing Belarus, Bulgaria and Luxembourg.

Fast forward 24 months and a League A place was secured on the final night of an unbeaten campaign that had looked a serious challenge. Prior the trip to Georgia in September, Northern Ireland had a record of played 10, lost eight and drawn two at this level.

Across six games in eight weeks, the side won three and drew three, conceding just once along the way. Justin Devenny's brilliant equaliser in Budapest on Tuesday secured the draw required to top the group and earn promotion to the top tier for the first time.

"It's a great feeling for us," said manager Michael O'Neill.

"In a way, we sort of set out when we came into this level of competition first of all to try and stay in it. Obviously to do that, you have to, you have to be in the top two positions.

"We knew that would help us potentially with the draw for Euro 2028, but to come away unbeaten, to to finish top and to give the performances we've given against some very good teams, it's very encouraging."

Northern Ireland draw with Hungary to seal Nations League promotion

Watch: Devenny the hero as NI earn promotion

Belief and bench help NI scale new heights

With such a young team on the international stage, there will always be more intangible markers of progress too.

It is perhaps easy to forget that not so long ago Pierce Charles' shot-stopping was being questioned as a weaker part of his game when compared to his range of passing.

Quite apart from a penalty save from Georgia's Khvicha Kvaratskhelia to start the group, the Queens Park Rangers loanee made momentum-changing stops from Georges Mikautadze in Tbilisi and Danylo Sikan against Ukraine in Belfast last week.

All three of the Ruairi McConville-Tom Atcheson-Ciaron Brown defensive triumvirate have enhanced their reputations too, while, perhaps not yet in a first-choice XI, Patrick Kelly, Terry Devlin and Brodie Spencer feel increasingly trusted members of a deepening pool of players.

O'Neill may have only made nine changes to his starting teams across the six games, but it felt notable how often those coming in did not look out of place.

The bench then made a huge impact in the draw that sealed promotion with two players at opposing ends of the experience spectrum, Josh Magennis and Kieran Morrison, to the fore.

"We have a squad now where I think the younger players are coming in and making an impact straight away," said O'Neill, who still has the likes of Conor Bradley and Daniel Ballard to come back into his squad.

"Which is a real positive, particularly if you look at the likes of Tom and Ruairi, but also Kieran Morrison and Ceadach [O'Neill] coming in and getting minutes early in their international careers.

"I just think that like the the squad is still very much still in development, we we still have areas where we still need to improve."

The next staging post for this Northern Ireland team is the Euro2 2028 qualifying draw in Belfast on 6 December.

They will be among the second seeds thanks to their top-two Nations League finish and, depending on whether they land in a four or five-team group, could face qualifiers as early as March next year.

"The great thing is we finish on a high and the players go back to their clubs now, they have to deal with a lot of football, they're all playing and demanding leagues," added O'Neill.

"Depending on the draw, we might come in in March and have friendly games which might be a nice thing for us to have.

"But we can just go in and see what the draw brings in December and then we can sort of plot the next 12 months."

After a string of impressive performances in the past three months, they will do so with serious momentum.

West Ham’s £18m Castellanos Problem Could Prove Difficult To Solve

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Reports originating from Argentine journalist Renzo Pantich have suggested that Taty Castellanos wants to leave West Ham United and has already rejected a lucrative offer from Flamengo in favour of remaining in Europe.

Where in Europe, one may ask – and that’s a question no one knows!

Pantich’s claim has generated plenty of attention, but when you dig beneath the surface, it raises a lot of questions!

The suggestion appears to be that by rejecting Flamengo, Castellanos has demonstrated a desire to leave West Ham while remaining in Europe. Yet that conclusion ignores several important realities.

Firstly, Flamengo are reportedly facing financial difficulties with high transfer debts and sponsorship issues due to the Brazilian government banning football clubs from receiving payments from betting and online casinos.

The Brazilian midfielder reportedly accepted a sizeable wage reduction to join Flamengo. It seems highly unlikely that Castellanos, who is approaching what should be the peak years of his career, would be prepared to make a similar sacrifice.

Flamengo’s interest in the striker is nothing new. The Rio giants admired him during his time at Lazio but were unable to make a deal happen because the finances simply didn’t work. The transfer fee and salary package required were beyond what they were prepared to pay. Indeed, it was suggested at the time the player wanted to remain in Europe – sounds like a déjà vu moment!?

Taty wants to leave but who can realistically afford him?

Lastly, and most importantly, there is no mention of any transfer discussions between West Ham and Flamengo on any news wires.

Nobody doubts the emotional attraction. Castellanos has never hidden his admiration for the Argentine giants, and a move back home would undoubtedly appeal on a personal level. The problem is affordability.

Matching a transfer fee acceptable to West Ham while also covering the striker’s wages would represent a huge financial commitment, even for one of South America’s biggest clubs. As C&H has previously reported, River Plate simply can’t afford the player.

In fact, C&H exclusively revealed that it is West Ham who have been actively exploring ways of finding a buyer for the underperforming forward rather than the player agitating for a move.

Despite accepting a wage reduction following West Ham’s relegation, Castellanos remains on a highly attractive contract. His current deal runs until 2030 and includes a one-year club option.

Even following the Championship wage reduction, he is still earning only marginally less than the reported £48,000 per week he received at Lazio.

Should West Ham return to the Premier League this season and want to activate that extension, Castellanos could still have effectively four years of Premier League-level earnings ahead of him.

At around £77,000 per week, that’s potentially close to £18 million in future wages.

Those are numbers very few clubs outside England’s top flight can realistically match.

There will almost certainly need to be compromise from both sides. West Ham appear keen to move the striker on, while Castellanos may have to accept that replacing those wages elsewhere will be extremely difficult.

For all the speculation, one thing seems clear: finding a buyer may prove considerably harder than finding rumours!

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.
